# ADR Cross-Reference Graph Audit - 2026-05-20

Output path: `docs/architecture/adr-cross-reference-graph-2026-05-20.md`
Audit mode: read-only against ADR corpus; this file is the only authored artifact.
Corpus root: all Markdown files under `docs/**` with basenames matching `ADR-NNNN*.md`.
Graph node model: normalized ADR ID (`ADR-NNNN`), aggregating duplicate/amendment files under the same ID.

## §1 Methodology

This audit builds a directed graph where `A -> B` means ADR-A cites ADR-B.

Edge detection rules:
1. Parse ADR-shaped files by filename pattern `ADR-NNNN*.md`; normalize every source file to its first `ADR-NNNN` ID.
2. Extract structured-array references from `related_adrs:`, `supersedes:`, `depends_on:`, and `amends:` fields, including YAML list rows and inline scalar rows.
3. Also recognize the repo-local `related:` array as an alias because many current ADRs use it instead of `related_adrs:`; evidence rows still preserve the detected field name.
4. Extract every inline `ADR-NNNN` mention from body, frontmatter, blockquotes, tables, and headings.
5. Deduplicate graph edges by normalized pair `(source ADR ID, target ADR ID)` while retaining raw mention counts and up to two evidence locations per catalog row.
6. Exclude self-cites from directed graph metrics; self mentions are counted only in raw mention totals.
7. Treat a target as existing when at least one ADR-shaped file has the same normalized `ADR-NNNN` basename.
8. Treat missing reverse cites mechanically: if `A -> B` exists and `B -> A` does not, the pair is cataloged. This is a completeness gap, not automatically a semantic defect.
9. Status drift is mechanical: any source ID with status set containing `accepted` citing any target ID with status set containing `superseded`.
10. Strongly-connected components use Tarjan over existing-target directed edges.
11. Cluster analysis uses high-centrality ADR seed neighborhoods plus shared citation overlap; each ADR is assigned to the seed with the strongest direct/backlink/shared-neighbor score.

Scope notes:
- Duplicate files with the same ADR number are aggregated. This matters for amendment pairs such as ADR-0246, ADR-0253, ADR-0255, and ADR-0257.
- Historical ADR-shaped files outside `docs/decisions` are included because the request asked for all ADR cross-references.
- Broken cites are normalized to the cited `ADR-NNNN`; filename slug mismatches are not treated as broken if the ID exists.

## §6 Cluster Analysis

Cluster method: choose high-centrality seed ADRs, reject near-duplicate seeds with neighborhood Jaccard > 0.42, then assign every ADR to the seed with the strongest direct-cite, reverse-cite, and shared-neighbor score. Density is `internal directed edges / n*(n-1)`.

Execution order:

1. Resolve broken target IDs before reverse-cite work. Broken targets create false sinks and distort load-bearing rank.
2. Resolve accepted-to-superseded drift next. These are likely to mislead implementers because the source ADR still looks authoritative.
3. Add reverse cites for the top load-bearing nodes only after deciding whether every cite family is intended to be bidirectional.
4. Keep this report as audit evidence; perform ADR edits in separate Oya VCS claims so this audit remains clean and reviewable.
5. Re-run the graph after every repair wave and compare `adrs:N edges:M broken:X` evidence to this baseline.

## §11 Verdict

The ADR graph is dense and broadly reachable: 258 normalized ADR IDs produce 3463 valid directed cross-reference edges, and strict isolated ADR count is 0.
The corpus is not final-clean: 31 broken target pairs, 24 accepted-to-superseded drift pairs, and 2763 missing reverse pairs remain.
The most load-bearing ADRs are architecture and doctrine anchors, especially ADR-0145, ADR-0131, ADR-0028, ADR-0105, ADR-0056, ADR-0009, ADR-0001, ADR-0132, ADR-0003, and ADR-0244.
The cite-density pass partitions the graph into 18 seed-neighborhood clusters; the biggest cleanup pressure is concentrated around ADR-0244 and ADR-0001 if reviewed by internal-edge count.
The highest-leverage cleanup is not broad manual backlinking. Start with broken target resolution, then drift correction, then reverse-cite policy for the top citation hubs.
Stop condition for this audit is met when this file exists, has at least 2,000 lines, reports the computed graph counts, and Oya VCS verify/done/promote succeed with matching evidence.
